## v164 Motion engine 2 — motion graphics drawn the way the big tools draw them
- Owner: "the motion graphics you made don't rise to the one I sent — the editor is weak; have you looked at the big companies?" Compared (public sources) with After Effects (graph-editor easing, motion blur by shutter angle, 3D camera + depth of field, glow), Jitter (easing presets incl. overshoot/elastic, custom curves, stagger, kinetic type), Cavalry (procedural duplicators/behaviours), Rive (bones, meshes, state machines). The v150 scenes had one easing, a glow, flat 2D, no camera, no motion blur and hard cuts.
- `mgDraw150` is now Motion engine 2 (v164 block): the scene draws on its own layer (`draw164`) under a camera (slow push-in, float), with a soft depth shadow (`shadow164`: the layer's shape at 1/8 size, scaled up), a light glint once it has come in (`glint164`, source-atop), and its way in/out (`trans164`): at a cut between two scenes both sides use the same kind — zoom-through, whip or flash — chosen from the cut's time (`CUT164`); the first scene fades/scales in, the last fades out. Live preview leaves two faint copies on whips; the export uses real motion blur.
- Motion blur (export only, `R139.on`): where things move fast (first 1.4 s, last 0.45 s of a scene) the background is drawn once and the scene's layer at `MG164.N`=6 sub-frames across a 180° shutter, added with "lighter" at 1/N (an exact average, also where the layer is see-through); holds are one picture, no extra work.
- Text: `kin164` — letters come in on a spring (`spring164`, ~12 % past the mark) with squash & stretch; Arabic and other joined scripts word by word (the finished line is pixel-identical to plain text). Sparks (`burst164`) when the logo, a counter's number and the end card land. Phone, leaderboard and call cards turn in 3D (a skew-scale transform) with a contact shadow (`SC164`).
- Background `bg150` (also the ad's background clip): painted at 1/3 size and scaled up (bilinear) — brand colour + two neighbours drifting as soft light (`hue164`), a glowing perspective floor grid, bokeh at three depths, vignette; grain (overlay) on top. An ad's middle scenes take palette colours next to the brand's (`sceneAc164`); logo, trail and end keep the brand colour.
- Speed: a 1080×1920 frame ≈ 40 ms in software rendering (was 109 ms before the low-res background/shadow); the v150 export test runs in ~90 s (was 187 s before v164).
- Tests: `tests/v164.py` (port 8498: spring, matched cuts, scenes still between cuts, palette, blur where moving and none on a hold, lit background, Arabic joined, Latin letters in order, every scene draws incl. export path).

## v165 A living face and an acted voice for talking avatars
- Asked: "The permanent eyes are permanent and strange — an engine that works with the lip engine, so the facial expressions are realistic, with realistic voice acting like ElevenLabs, in harmony with the whole site." Found: a talking avatar from a photo (Kling AI Avatar / InfiniteTalk) was sent with an empty prompt (Kling) or "A person talking naturally" (InfiniteTalk), and with a voice file the prompt was the file's name; the voice was one flat Multilingual v2 read. Those engines move the face the way they are told — told nothing, the photo's open eyes stay on the lens.
- `lib/face165.js` (pure; the text AI is loaded only when used): `plan165(words, {lang, tone, ai})` → `{beats [{i, text, emo, cue, t0, t1}], by: ai|rules, tone, dur, face, voiceText}`. Lines = sentences (`sentences165`); each line's feeling from the text AI as acting director (`direct165`: only picks from `EMO165` and a `cue` flag) else from the words (`cue165`: laughter, angry, sad, whisper, a question, surprise, greetings/thanks, "!", a pause "…" — English and Arabic); a chosen tone (`toneEmo165`) fills lines with no feeling of their own. Times from words per second by language (`lineDur165`).
- The face direction (`faceDirection165`, English, ≤ 2400 chars): `LIVING165` (real blinking every 2–5 s and at the end of sentences, never a frozen stare; eyes that move — a glance away to think, back to the lens on the key words; brows, cheeks, small nods and tilts, breathing; lips exact; same face/background) + "Acting, in order: 1) 0–1.9 s, warm: …". InfiniteTalk gets `faceShort165` (< 700 chars). The voice text (`voiceText165`) carries at most three ElevenLabs v3 audio tags ([laughs] [sighs] [whispers] [excited] [curious]) on lines whose own words show the feeling; `plainText165` takes them out.
- `providers/kie.js` (`perform165`, `planFor165`, `lipVoice`/`makeVoice165`): every photo avatar job (kling/ai-avatar-*, infinitalk/*) gets the direction as its prompt and, with words, the acted voice: the site's ElevenLabs key → `speak(…, {expressive: true})` (eleven_v3 with tags; falls back to v2 inside `speak`); else the AI engine's `elevenlabs/text-to-dialogue-v3` (`{dialogue:[{text, voice id}], stability: 0.5}` — field names from search results, docs.kie.ai is blocked here: verify on the first real run), refused (400/404/422) → off for 6 h, a failed task → 1 h (`V3_165.offUntil`); then Multilingual v2 / Turbo 2.5 / the default voice with the words without tags and the plan's tone (`ttsFeel`), then the backup voice. A voice file: no voice made, the living direction (never the file's name). A face video (Volcengine lips) gets the acted voice and no direction (its own eyes). Plans and voices are kept 3 h (`PLAN165`, `VOICE165`): a job tried again on the next face engine (`KIE_FALLBACK.lipsync`) reuses the voice — not made or paid twice.
- Site-wide: `lib/speech160.js speechWords` — every talking video's prompt now asks for "natural blinking and living eyes (never a fixed stare)". Page block `v165`: an "Acting" chip in the Home avatar box (sheet `actTiles165`: Auto · Warm · Excited · Serious · Calm · Funny · Sad · Whisper, with icons `ACTIC165`) and the same row in the Voice page's lip-sync panel; `S.hf.act165` → the lipsync job's `meta.tone` (Auto sends none; a tone already set stays).
- Tests: `tests/v165.py` (ports 8493/8494; a stand-in engine that makes the voices and the videos, refuses v3 or fails the face engine on request, and a stand-in text AI as the acting director). `tests/v81_calm.py` accepts the v3 dialogue request (the same voice id and words).

## v168 Slow motion in the editor is smooth by itself
- Asked: "Slow motions in the editor". Measured first: a clip slowed to 0.25× in the editor showed each of its frames 5 times (the export's frames matched the true moments only 40 % of the time, against a test pattern drawn at 120 fps); the device's optical-flow frames help but run ~17 ms late and still hold some frames.
- Server (`lib/slowmo163.js`): the editor's own slow motion — `meta.slow163:"ed"` with `speed163` (0.1–<1) and `ramp163` (none/in/out/bullet) — is `edSpec163`; `edWarp163` times it exactly as the editor does (speed as a function of the clip's place on the timeline, `edxRampW`), so the file is as long as the editor's clip (2 s at 0.25× → 8 s; a bullet ramp → the same 3.2 s the editor shows). Free (`slowFinish163` → 0, like Reverse: this server's ffmpeg, no AI engine paid). A speed or ramp the editor doesn't have is refused (400), never made as a paid preset; only the client's own video (404).
- Page (v168 part of the v163 block): slowing a clip smooths it on the device at once and, once the speed has stayed the same 1.4 s (a page drawing meanwhile doesn't put it off), starts the engine's version (`slowEd163`, a Finish job, cost 0). When it is ready the clip is swapped for it (`edit163`: `c.sl0` keeps the original; speed 1, same length); the panel says so with "Back to the original clip" (`sl163back`); a new speed (chips / slider, window capture listeners) brings the original back first and starts again. A video the server refuses is told once (`c.no163`) and stays smoothed on the device. The export (`prep163` before `edRender139`) starts what is missing, waits for the engine's files (≤10 min, the button says so), then the device's frames.
- Two export bugs found while measuring (both affected every export): (1) any `renderView` during an export (a job finishing, a toast) replaced the preview canvas the export records from — the rest of the video froze on one picture; now the canvas is kept in place while `R139.keep/on` (set from the export's first step). (2) WebM keeps times in whole milliseconds (0.067 for 0.0667), and the frame lookup (`at139`, 0.1 ms slack) showed every third frame of a 30 fps WebM twice; now 1 ms.
- Result (tests/v168.py): the export of a 0.25× clip — 3 % still frames, 97 % at their true moment (was 35 % / 40 %).
- Tests: `tests/v168.py` (port 8492; the test browser can't decode H.264, so the engine's file is handed to it as VP9 through a route with byte ranges). `tests/v163.py`'s editor checks now expect the free automatic version.

## v169 The editor: captions that are the words said, a playhead that always moves, a clean opening; 9 agents in a row
- Captions on the device (`edxAutoCaps`, v169 block): the small on-device speech model invented words over silence and music ("Thank you.", subscribe / subtitle-credit lines, "[Music]"), looped phrases and drifted from the voice. Now the sound of the edit is read as voice stretches (`env169`: loudness every 20 ms, dips < 120 ms bridged, clicks < 60 ms dropped) and `clean169` drops tags (brackets, ♪), credit lines (`JUNK169`, EN + AR), words with no voice within 0.6 s, lone stock endings over music (`ENDS169`), loops (`unloop169`), then `align169` lays each word on the voice stretch it overlaps (never going back in time; a long stretch of sound keeps its words where they were heard). The language heard in the first part is passed to the next parts (`detect149`).
- Exact captions (`lib/captions169.js`, `GET /v1/captions169?dur` → price, `POST /v1/captions169 {url, lang}`): the page uploads the edit's own 16 kHz WAV; ElevenLabs Scribe (word times) → OpenAI Whisper with `timestamp_granularities[]=word` (`transcribeWords` in lib/localize.js). Priced per minute (`locPrice().stt`, staff free) and shown on the button first; held → charged when words come back, released on failure or when nothing is said; someone else's file refused; the WAV is deleted after. The panel shows "Exact captions · N credits" + "On this device · free" when the server has speech-to-text, else the device button only. Typed words are laid on the voice (`spreadVoice169`).
- Playhead (edited in place in the TLX listeners, line ~8443): on the playhead's line (mouse ±5 px, finger ±14 px) — even over a clip — it is the playhead you take. The mouse at once; a finger waits for its first move: sideways → playhead (long-press timer and clip drag cancelled, the page doesn't scroll), up/down → the lanes scroll, still → a tap picks the clip / a long press opens its menu as before. A picked clip's edge under the playhead still trims with the mouse.
- The editor opens on Media with nothing picked (`open169` via a `go` wrapper and at boot) unless the way in asked for a panel (a result sent to the editor, captions from the Subtitles page…); inside the editor a chosen panel stays.
- Home → Agents: the 9 agents in one row (sliding sideways on a phone) and the plan as a row of 9 cards in the same order (the Editor had been left out of a one-shot plan). There are 9 agents — Quill, Nova, Max, Lumi, Lyra, Echo, Remy, Duke, Iris.
- The editor's Export button is an icon only on phones (≤767 px; its name in `aria-label`/`title`).
- Tests: `tests/v169.py` (ports 8495/8496: stand-in Scribe/Whisper; phone with CDP touches + desktop). `tests/equal_layout.py` skips the plan row (`.hbls`) like other sideways rows.
- v169b (from the owner's check-up): the "out of credits" mark (`alerts138`) is taken away by any check that finds the engine answering with credits on its balance — `record()` in lib/health.js, so "Test all" and the 15-minute check clear it too (before: only a finished job or the one-engine Test, so it stayed on for hours after a top-up). A balance the check can't read now says why (`balance not read (HTTP 404)` / the engine's own message) instead of a bare "OK", keeps the mark, and the check-up tells the owner to press Test on the AI engine after topping up. Tested in `tests/v138.py` (4b).

## v170 The Home box empties after Create · no overlap in the box · a fast editor playhead and picture
- After Create (`hfSend` wrapper, v170 block): when a job was really added, `empty170(tab)` takes out the face photo (`hfRef`), the angle photos (`hfAng1-3`), the voice (`hfAud`) and, on Video, the # pictures (`REF130` slots, `S.draft.refs130`), start/end frames and the start job — from `M`, `S.mediaKeys` and the device store (`IDB.del`), so a reload doesn't bring them back. Nothing made (refused, no credits, empty words) → the box keeps everything. The talking-avatar-with-angles path (face first, then the video) keeps the voice for its second step and empties when that step's job is added. The words were already cleared (v88).
- Overlap: a tall angle photo no longer stretches its tile over the Acting chip — the picture fills its tile (`.hfang img`/`.hfref.has img` absolute, `object-fit:cover`), its × above it.
- Editor speed: the main loop only draws the editor picture when something changed (`draw170`: playing, recording, exporting, a pointer down, the playhead held, a timeline drag → every frame; else a change of time, project, video frame readiness, sprite strip, or any input/media/render event marks it; at most 700 ms between draws). An idle editor no longer redraws 60 times a second, so the page answers a finger at once. The playhead moves by `transform: translate3d` (no layout), and the timeline doesn't follow-scroll while the playhead is held. Tests that change things behind the page's back and wait frames set `DR170.dirty=true`.
- The "newer project on your other device" check (`sync149`) waits while a finger is down on a tile (`DND144.s`) or the playhead (`TLX.s`) — its banner re-drew the editor in the middle of a long-press drag.
- Tests: `tests/v170.py` (port 8500: Home box phone + desktop, editor draw counts idle / jump / playing). `tests/v144.py` re-finds the tile after scrolling; `tests/v152.py` marks the picture changed; `tests/v154.py` accepts the icon-only Export (v169); `tests/v156.py` waits for db.json.
